Sediment transport capaciÿ relationship : [SOURCE: Page_81]

First, RubarBE software used the classical relationship that (Meyer-Peter and Müller, 1948) proposed for bed load transport: [SOURCE: Page_81]

[SOURCE: Page_81]

For practical use, this relationship can be modified considering a change in the value of the parameters, particularly the non-dimensional ones: critical shear stress that is set at 0.047 in equation (4) and the multiplying factor of capaciÿ that is set I at in equation (4). Other relationships have been also introduced to take into account the cases in which suspended load should also considered: the one [SOURCE: Page_81]

78 [SOURCE: Page_81]

proposed by (Bagnold, 1966) and the one proposed by (Engelund and Hansen, 1967). [SOURCE: Page_82]

Spatial lag equation [SOURCE: Page_82]

(5)               ?s dx C,- Q, D"ho, in which D"1r,is the distance that characterizes the abiliÿ of sediment transport to reach the value of the sediment transport capaciÿ. For bed load transport in rivers, this value is generally very short (a few meters), which means that it is shorter than the space step and thus can be neglected. The method for solving the set of equations is based on several steps. First, de Saint Venant equations are solved by a Godunov ÿpe second order finite difference scheme that makes possible the calculation of flow variables even if critical flow appears (Paquier, 1995). Then, the sediment transport capaciÿ is calculated. Solving the spatial lag equation inside a cell is carried out according the scheme shown on figure 1, which calculates the sediment discharge downstream Qso" from the upstream sediment discharge Qs'", while distinguishing the sediments that are only transferred (Qsto) to the ones that are interfering witn tne sediments previously present in the cell (deposited Qsd"p and eroded Qs"t) . Figure 1. Scheme for spatial lag calculation @alayn,2001) [SOURCE: Page_82]

Then the sediment continuiÿ (equation (3)) is applied to every cell on the basis of figure 2. This leads to a change of As that should be translated as a change of the shape of the cross section. This change will then change the water level as the hypothesis of no change in the water depth and velociÿ is applied. ln figure 2, the active layer that corresponds to the sediment that is moving during the time step has its thickness fixed from the sediment transport capaciÿ, the velociÿ of the flow and the space step. The deposits and erosions occurs when this active layer is respectively too thick or not thick enough. Thus, inside one cell, a sedimentary compartment corresponds to a set of sediments that have a coherent behaviour and three compartments are defined: [SOURCE: Page_82]

79 [SOURCE: Page_82]

- A compartment MM of movable sediments: the contents of the water column where particles are moving at one time. We distinguish a compartment M", of input sediments and a compartment M", of output sediments. - A compartment A of the active layer: a layer near the bed where sediment particles slide or roll at one point during the time step. - A compartment B of one or several substrate layers: it reflects historical deposition of sediments on the riverbed or undisturbed subsurface. Some of the layers can be created or can disappear. [SOURCE: Page_83]

The simplest one-dimensional models represent sediments only by a mean diameter Dso. This simplification clearly does not fully describe the processes that occur in many channels (armouring, presence of mixed sediments of various sizes, etc). Therefore, a complementary parameter was added: the standard deviation o (defined by the square root of the ratio between D64 and D16), which appears convenient to describe grain size distribution in a river for which sediments are homogeneous (Shih and Komar, 1990). Extra parameters of one compartment are the shear stress rmn at beginning of the movement and q, shear stress at the end of the movement. Generally, these two last parameters are set equal and determined from D5e. During the phases 1 to 4 of figure 2, sediments are mixed or [SOURCE: Page_83]

[SOURCE: Page_84]

5. The example of lower lsère river The case concems a 3 kilometres long stretch of the River lsère immediately upstream of the junction between the lsère and the canalised reach of the River Rhône (Fig. 3). The downstream boundary is the water level upstream from the regulation dam. This water level is lowered only during floods. Because of such conditions, a huge volume of sediments (about 650 000 m^3) has deposited in the downstream part of the river. The question was: will these deposits be flushed during a high flood? lf not, the calculation of water levels shows that the banks and may be the roads could be overflowed. [SOURCE: Page_84]

81 [SOURCE: Page_84]

{ [SOURCE: Page_85]

3u [SOURCE: Page_85]

I [SOURCE: Page_85]

Figure 3 Situation map of lower Isère River. The modelled reach was between Chambons and the junction. [SOURCE: Page_85]

To obtain information about what might happen, a laboratory model was built by the CNR (National Company of Rhône) at scale of 1:100 with the sediments represented by maize powder of densiÿ equal to 1.04 and diameter of 0.4 mm. The volume of flushed sediments was measured in eight short sections of the river in the downstream area. From these laboratory tests, it appears (CNR, 1999) that for both the 100 year and the 1000 year floods, the sediments are strongly removed during the beginning of the water increase, so that the water levels will remain relatively low. To confirm this conclusion, RubarBE software was used, but at the field scale (Balayn, 2001), not at the scale of the laboratory. This was because an adaptation of the numerical model should have been necessary and would not have proved the validiÿ of the results at the field scale. Thus, actual diameter of the sediments oi 0.32 mm and densiÿ equal to 2.6 were used. From the cross sections measured in 1995 (not far from present topography), a topographical model of the river bed was built. lnitial cross sections, and some other ones interpolated to reach a space step of about 50 metres (maximum: 100 m), were used for the 1-D calculations. [SOURCE: Page_85]

83 [SOURCE: Page_86]

ln the first step, called the calibration phase, it was checked that the morphology of the river bed after the 1000 year flood was similar to the laboratory models in the calculation results. After calibration of the Strickler coefficient, the porosiÿ and of the active width, the maximum difference for the eight volumes measured in the laboratory was 21 % while globally the difference was 8 %. Results in terms of water level and bed elevation are shown on figure 4. Similar results were obtained for the 100 years'flood but with a lower global difference (4%) and with higher differences downstream (up to 41%). [SOURCE: Page_87]

Thus, it was necessary to confirm the results obtained by the numerical model by checking it on observations at the field scale. Modelling the deposition during the period 1968-1995 appeared to be the only available solution. Although, the processes were not exactly the same ones because of dominant deposition instead of dominant erosion, sediment ÿpes were the same and the boundary conditions could be correctly assessed by lengthening the stretch by several kilometres in the upstream conditions and by the operating rules of the dam for the downstream boundary. However, the calculation for the whole period lasted for too long and it was decided to limit the calculation to the 350 days of higher discharges (> 500 m^3/s). This calculation shows (figure 5) that the longitudinal profile is similar to the observed evolution, but lower. The volume of sediments amounts only to 70 % of the observed total (Balayn, 2001). Finally, by considering that on the period the discharges over 300 m^3/s (lasting 1950 days) willalso carry sediments (on the field site, limit of movement was observed to be between 200 and 300 m^3/s), it appears that both the volume and the longitudinal profile obtained were suitable to consider that the model can be trusted. Thus, although these calculations did not demonstrate the accuracy of the conclusions, they add weight to the idea of the sediment being flushed away in large enough quantities so as not to cause an overflow of water. [SOURCE: Page_87]

## CONCLUSIONS

A 1-D model can be used to treat various sediment transport problems in rivers, particularly those conceming the sediment management at the scale of a river basin. Due to the varieÿ of the questions and of the sediment processes, it is important to use a set of relevant equations to model the main process and to obtain an operational answer. Rubar BE soffware was developed to treat the bed material load transport in cases in which both the flow and the evolution are rapid. Stabiliÿ of the calculations and validation on simple cases had previously proved that classical methods have been integrated in the software in a suitable way. ln natural conditions, these very transient problems are generally met in mountainous or hilly areas. ln such cases, the field experience demonstrates the importance of the modelling of the evolution of the shape of the cross sections, and on the representation of the sediment sorting. The ways to integrate these capabilities were original: - on the one hand, introducing a simplified geometrical calculation of the local shear stress that will determine the relative level of deposition or erosion; [SOURCE: Page_87]

84 [SOURCE: Page_87]

- on the other hand, the introduction of the standard deviation of the sediment diameters instead of a division of the sediments in several classes. Until now, the two methods have brought interesting results but have not demonstrated neither their validiÿ in field problems nor their applicability for sediment management questions. [SOURCE: Page_88]

English summary The aim of the paper is to present a numerical simulation of sediment transport in a mountainous river. Two one-dimensional sediment transport and riverbed evolution models Rubarbe and Metoda are used for predicting the variation of longitudinal bed profile along the river reach and changes in the cross-sectional geometry due to erosion or deposition of sediment. ln the cunent paper, simulation of the sediment transport is applied to the Raba River a   - mountiain tributary of the Wisla River. The simulation of riverbed evolution is applied for the reach located from 81.829 kmlo^77.751km (upstream from the Dobczyce Dam), about 20 km to the south of Krakow. The results of the simulation from the two models are analysed and discussed. [SOURCE: Page_89]

Résumé français z Un modèle unidimensionnel de transport de sédiments et son applicatîon à une rivière de montagne, la Raba. Le but de cette communication est de présenter les simulations numériques du transport de sédiments dans une rivière de montagne. Deux modèles unidimensionnels simulant le transport de sédiments et l'évolution du lit, les modèles RubarBE et Metoda sont utilisés pour prédire la variation du profil en long du lit et les changements de la géométrie des profils en travers dus à l'érosion ou au dépôt de sédiments. Dans notre communication, ces modèles sont appliqués à la Raba, un affluent de la Vistule. Le bief étudié s'étend du PK 81,829 au PK 77,751 (à I'amont du banage Dobczyce),20 kilomètres environ au Sud de Cracovie. Les résultats des calculs par les deux modèles sont analysés et discutés. ,l. Presentation of the Raba River [SOURCE: Page_90]

The Raba river, in southem Poland, is a mountain tributary of the \Msla River. ln this region, the topography of drainage catchments is highly varied. The division of the Carpathian rivers courses is as follows: the upper course (with the average slopes: 7 % - 15 960), the middle and the lower courses (with the average slopes 2.5%- 0.5 %o). In the Carpathian rivers a high varieÿ of water stages is observed: a rapidgrowing flow appears especially in spring and early summer while, in the period of dry season or during the longJasting snow-cover on rivers, a low-flow period can be observed. ln the Raba river, during the flood, discharge can reach the value Q = 800 m^3/s in the upper course, and àbout Q = 1500 mt/J in the lower course. The total catchment area of the Raba River is 1537.10 km^2. At Km 60.00 of the River course, the Dobczyce dam is located. [SOURCE: Page_90]

The Raba River is characterized by an erosion and deposition process which occurs with varied intensity along the river course. However, the main erosion process is observed downstream from the Dobczyce dam (Maÿas and Lapuszek, 2000). [SOURCE: Page_90]

2.Presentation of the experimental reach ln the current paper, simulation of the sediment transport and riverbed evolution is applied for the reach which is located (Fig. 2) from 81.829 km to 77.751 km (upstream from the Dobczyce Dam). The experimental reach is located about 30 km to the south of Krakow. The experimental reach is closed to the important road Krak^6w - Zakopane. The river in the presented reach was repeatedly straightened and narrowed during the 20'n century. The hydraulic engineering activity is the most important factor of the strong erosion of riverbed in the mentioned reach of the Raba River (Ratomski and \Mtowska, 1993). The observations show that up to 3 metres of bed degradation has occurred since the beginning of the engineering activiÿ (Fig. 1). [SOURCE: Page_90]

A few years ago a project of extension of the international road situated next to the river Raba was set up. Leading to a partial replacement and narrowing of the riverbed along the road is applied (see table 1, Fig. 3). The engineering activiÿ on the experimental reach started in spring 2003. Work is due to be completed at the end of October 2003. At this point morphology of the evolution of the Raba River will be examined. [SOURCE: Page_90]

### 3. The RubarBE and METODA models

The aim of this paper is to present the numerical simulation of sediment transport in the mountainous river. Two one-dimensional sediment transport and riverbed evolution models are used: METODA and RubarBE. 1-D model: RUBARBE has been developed by Cemagref; it is described in full in the article "What are the problems to be solved by a '1-D river sediment transport model ? Example of RubarBE software" (Paquier, 2003). [SOURCE: Page_93]

3.l.Description of the METODA model The one - dimensional model METODA was used for predicting variation of longitudinal riverbed profile along the Raba River reach, and for predicting changes in the cross-sectional geometry. This model describes the changes in river morphology due to erosion or deposition of sediment (Piwowarczyk Ogorek, 2003), (Piwowarczyk Ogorek et al., 2000). The model is based on the system of the following equations: . The Saint Venant equations for water: [SOURCE: Page_93]

Computational results obtained by METODA and RUBARBE are verified by field observations canied out in year 2001, before the project realisation. The experimental reach is 4 km long with 44 cross-sections taken into computation. However, for simplicity, table 2 contains verification performed only for the selected 20 cross-sections in which clear conclusions have been established in the field. The agreement between results is such that erosion and deposition are located in the same cross-sections with the same order of magnitude. [SOURCE: Page_100]

6.Gonclusion For a mountainous stream such as the Raba River, it is important to localise erosion or deposition areas, particularly after the work were carried out. Metoda and Rubarbe, two 1-D sediment transport models solving similar equations provide similar trends on the test case of a 4 km Raba reach. The differences are lower with 44 cross sections than with 19 cross sections as the differences in numerical schemes are less significant with smaller section width. These trends of erosion and deposition correspond to the field observations although more accurate comparison would have required both full topographies at two dates as well as discharges during the period between these dates. Finally, the parallel use of the two models tends to validate the accuracy of the numerical schemes and of the results, at least in terms of trends. [SOURCE: Page_100]

'Krakow Universiÿ of Technology, lnstitute of Water Engineering and Management, ul. Warszawska 24,31-155 Krakow, Poland, Tel. +48 12 628 28 48, Fax + Æ '12 628 20 41 alenar@snoek tl!§.pk.edupl ffiHydraulicsResearchUnit,3bisQuaiChauveau,cP22o,69336 Lyon, Cedex 09, France, Tel. + 33 47220 86 11, Fax. + 33 4 78 47 7875 andre. [EMAIL_REDACTED], [EMAIL_REDACTED] [SOURCE: Page_101]

English summâry Dam construction influences the form of a channel upstream and downstream from a hydraulic structure. Erosion is the main process downstream from each structure. The objec{ive of this paper is to present computational method to define the degradation process in the case of cutting off the sediment transport by a dam. Two one-dimensional sediment transport and riverbed evolution models Rubarbe and Model SSDD (Sediment Sorting Downstream ftom a Dam) were used. These models were applied to a 6 km reach of the Raba River in Poland downstream from the Dobczyce Dam. The difference between the models consists in water flow equations: in Rubarbe the full Saint Venant equations are used while the SSDD model is based on simplified flow equation. However the most important difference between the two models is, in SSDD, the modelling of sediment sorting during the erosion process. Therefore the calculation results differ significantly. The results of SSDD were compared with the field measurements of sediment diameter changes and the concordance was satisfactory. [SOURCE: Page_101]

Résumé français : Un modèle de transport de sédiments unidimensionnel et son application à l'érosion à l'aval d'un barrage (Dobczyce). [SOURCE: Page_101]

La construc{ion d'un banage influence la forme du chenal à I'amont et à I'aval de I'ouvrage. A l'aval, l'érosion est le processus majeur. L'objectif de cette communication est de présenter une méthode de calcul du processus d'érosion dans le cas d'une interception des sédiments par un barrage. Deux modèles unidimensionnels de transport de sédiments et d'évolution du lit RubarBE et SSDD sont utilisés. Ces modèles sont appliqués à un bief de 6 kilomètres de la Raba à I'aval du banage Dobczyce. Une différence entre les deux modèles réside dans les équations du fluide: équations de Saint Venant complètes pour RubarBE et simplifiées pour SSDD. Cependant la différence la plus importante entre les deux modèles est I'utilisation dans SSDD du tri granulométrique pendant le processus d'érosion. De ce fait, les résultats obtenus diffèrent fortement. Les résultats de SSDD comparés aux mesures de terrain en terme de granulométrie sont jugés satisfaisants. [SOURCE: Page_101]

98 [SOURCE: Page_101]

Streszczenie polskie: Zastosowanie programu l-D Transportu Rumowiska do przewidywania erozji poni^2ej zapory (Dobczyce) [SOURCE: Page_102]

Budowla hydrotechniczna pzegradzajqca zeke wptywa na morfologie koryta tej zeki zarôwno powyzej jak i poni^2ej swojego polo^2enia. Glôwnym procesem poni^2ej ka^2dej zapory i jest erozja miejscowa erozja na dlugoéci. Tematem pracy jest pzedstawienie metody i obliczeniowej, kt^6ra mo^2e okre§lié zdefiniowaé ten niszczqcy proces w warunkach calkowitego odcigcia doplywu rumowiska        z g^6rnego stanowiska budowli. W pracy zastosowano dwa rô2ne, jednowymiarowe modele opisujqce zmiany zachodzqce w korycie zeki, Rubarbe i model SSDD (Sediment Sorting Downstream ftom a Dam). Modele te zostaÿ zastosowane dla 6 km odcinka zeki Raby poni^2ej zapory w Dobczycach. Podstawowa rô2nica pomiçdzy modelami polega na ÿm, 2e Rubarbe opisuje pzeplyr wody za pomocq pehrych r^6wnaÉ St. Venanta, natomiast SSDD wykozystuje ich uproszczonq formç. W modelu SSDD przyjeto rôwnie^2 innq interpretacje samego procesu erozji poprzez wprowadzenie sortowania rumowiska i mo^2e byé to pow^6d znacznych rô2nic w otrzymanych wynikach. \Âfniki SSDD wykazujq zgodno§ô z wynikami pomiarôw poziomu dna i §rednicy rumowiska. [SOURCE: Page_102]

1. Erosion downstream from a dam Dam construction influences the form of a channel upstream and downstream from a hydraulic structure. The erosion downstream from a dam is composed of two phenomena - local scour and riverbed degradation along important length of a river reach. The latter is a long-duration and longdistance phenomenon. This paper presents one case of riverbed erosion, the degradation downstream from the Dobczyce Dam on the Raba River. [SOURCE: Page_102]

The Raba River is a mountainous tributary of the Vÿisla River in Poland. The river is located about 30 km to the south of Krakôw (Fig. 1). The river in the cross-section located close to the dam is characterized by the following parameters: 2 - catchments atea: 768 km - Q,or.= 1260 m'§ - Qo,ey"= 1560 m]/s - Qo,ry"= 1900 m"{s - Qo,osvo= 2700 m"/s [SOURCE: Page_102]

ln 1974-87 years the Dobczyce Dam was constructed at km 60 of the river. Maximum dam height is 30.6 m. The main purpose of the construction was water supply for Krakéw. The dam cuts off upstream bed load transport completely. [SOURCE: Page_102]

Slow degradation of the Raba riverbed was observed during the last 100 years. Figure 2 presents evolution of the riverbed during the previous century. The rate of erosion during the first 60 years was about 1,7 cm per year. The river bed changes were intensified by human activities: deforestation, which reduces retention abiliÿ of the catchment area, changes in agriculture and the urbanization. The next reason for riverbed degradation is hydraulic engineering activities, for example [SOURCE: Page_103]

100 [SOURCE: Page_103]

river training (Ratomski and \Mtowska, 1993). Raba river was trained during 1855 - 1976 years. The most important factor of rapid riverbed deepening was also floods. ïhe peak floods of exceptional magnitude in 1960 and 1972 as well as engineering structures accelerated erosion process. After 1972, trends of degradation was increasing. ln this period of time, the Dobczyce dam started to influence the channel downstream. [SOURCE: Page_104]

2. SSDD model The objective of the current paper is describing the erosion process downstream from the dam. The Authors used two one-dimensional models. Rubarbe is a model developed in Cemagref and is described in a companion paper (Paquier, 2003). Second model, is SSDD (Sediment Softing Downstream of a Daml. This model was developed in the lnstitute of Water Management and Engineering in Krakôw. The main purpose of this modelwas to describe the degradations process caused by cutting off the sediment transport by a dam. lt is very important that SSDD modeljoins the degradation process by the sediment sorting phenomena. Process of erosion can be expressed by four basic equations. The first two ones describe the fluid flow and they are kinematics wave flow equations, composed by: [SOURCE: Page_104]

Continuiÿ equation: ao aA a,*at=90 [SOURCE: Page_104]

Dynamic equation (Chezy formula): [SOURCE: Page_104]

The difference between the models used is the difference in water flow equations: in Rubarbe the full Saint Venant equations are used while the SSDD model is based on simplified flow equation. The next two equations describe the sediment movement and they are as follows: Sediment transport continuiÿ equation: [SOURCE: Page_104]

Sediment transport formula: t^3 roQs hr =o.o^4rr"d +r        r{;) q:'' where: [f)"' q'" - sediment flow[N's-1m-1]; k", lÇ - velociÿ coefficient in Manning, Strickler formulae [-]; Q - water discharge [m'/s], Q" - water dischargeàue t-o beginning of sediment movement [m^3/s], y, - specific weight of sediment, T,=2650 tkg'm-I. [SOURCE: Page_105]

ln Rubarbe and in the SSDD model, the Meyer-Peter-Müller sediment transport formula was used. SSDD model uses also variation of the Meyer-Peter-Müller one adapted for Carpathian mountain rivers. This formula contains coefficients taking into account sediment mixture and the blocking effect. {,5 p. g. h, . I -f, . g. [SOURCE: Page_105]

These equations are solved separately for every time step with appropriate initial and boundary conditions and for the following assumptions: -   the sediment transport cut-off caused by a dam, -   steady flow in the considered time step, -   no tributaries along the considered river reach. Presented computation method consists of following steps: -   solution of the water flow equations, -    sediment transport calculations for given cross-sections and slopes between them, -    establishing the new grain distribution curves based on sediment transport, -    establishing the new channel dimensions and a new slope. [SOURCE: Page_105]

A second difference is the way of computing slope. ln SSDD model, the initial slope is the measured slope of free water surface for Q = 100 mo/s. ln every time step, new bottom slope is calculated on the bases of the computed riverbed deformation. lt means that, after erosion or deposition process defined in each cross-section, a new rating curve is computed. On the base of the new curves, a new slope between the cross-sections is established. [SOURCE: Page_108]

ln the diagram, are shown initial bed, results from Rubarbe and four results from SSDD. The first result obtained by SSDD was based on water slope calculated by [SOURCE: Page_108]

105 [SOURCE: Page_108]

SSDD method. Change in the bed elevation was not high. ln next calculation, only initial water slope from Rubarbe was taken. Change in bed level was not big too. Third calculation is the case taking into account the slopes of water surface calculated by Rubarbe at every time step. ln this case, results obtained were somewhat different but not comparable with Rubarbe's results. The final computation was done with sediment sorting. Last result was different too. The verification of the computation results is very difficult because, in such a simplified case, there was no field measurements of riverbed level after the erosion process. However, the very different results obtained by Rubarbe seem linked to the simultaneous use of interpolated cross sections and sudden change in sediment diameter. [SOURCE: Page_109]

6. Gonclusion The models represent two different approaches to a detailed comparison. The main differences areas follows: - Erosion distribution in a cross-section - Number of cross-sections is different; in other words, in Rubarbe, cross-sections were added by interpolating geometry resulting in change of the geometry of the whole reach. Besides these computations for comparison canied out for unrealistic inflow hydrograph (for simpliciÿ reasons), the results obtained by SSDD model, for more realistic data corresponds to observed trends. Developments to reach a crossed validation of the two models requires: - Changes in SSDD model in the treatment of slope - The same cross-section treatment, which means same number of cross sections and same method for changing the shape, - lntroduction of sediment sorting to Rubarbe, - Computation for natural hydrograph, - Field measurements of river bed level. [SOURCE: Page_109]

Environmental and landscape aspects of mountainous stream channel development in the urbanised areas Jerzy RATOMSKI [SOURCE: Page_110]

Krakow Univers§ of Technology, lnstitute of Water Engineering and Management, ul Warszawska 24, 31-155 Krakow, Poland, Tel. +48 12 628 28 48, Fax + 48 12 628 20 41 [EMAIL_REDACTED] pl [SOURCE: Page_110]

English summâry lntensive development of many branches of economy involves the necessiÿ of ensuring safeÿ to people and infrastructure in the valleys of mountain streams. Heavy development of stream channel with material resistant to high stream velocities is often the only system. ln the paper, a short characteristic of the system with its advantages and disadvantages is made together with a critical review of solutions applied so far. Based on this, several new proposals to improve the functioning of the system are put forward. [SOURCE: Page_110]

Résumé français : Environnement et aspects du paysage au niveau de la cowtruction d'un canal d'une rivière de montagne dans les régions lourdement urbanisées [SOURCE: Page_110]

Le développement intensif de plusieurs branches de l'économie implique la nécessité d'assurer la sécurité des personnes et de l'infrastructure dans les vallées des rivières des montagnes. Un aménagement lourd des lits avec un renforcement des berges pour résister aux fortes crues était souvent la seule solution disponible. Dans ce document, un descriptif rapide du système ainsi que ses avantages et ses inconvénients ont été présentés, ainsi que des commentaires sur les solutions utilisées jusqu'à présent. Plusieurs nouvelles solutions sont proposées pour améliorer le fonctionnement du système. [SOURCE: Page_110]

W zwiqzku z intensywnym rozwojem wielu dziedzin gospodarki, bezpieczeÉstwo ludzi i zabezpieczenie infrastruktury w dolinach potokéw gôrskich stalo siç konieczno^6ciq. Jednym z systemôw, czçsto jedynym, jest ciç2ka techniczna obudowa koryta materialem odpornym na dziatanie du^2ych prqdko§ci. W arÿkule dokonano charakterysÿki systemu z uwypukleniem wad izalet oraz kryÿcznego pzeglqdu doÿchczas stosowanych rozvtiqzaÂ. Na tej podstawie zaproponowano szereg propozycji usprawniajqcych dzialanie systemu. [SOURCE: Page_110]

1. lntroduction Water resources have a[ways played an important role in human activities. Over the last century a rapid growth of population and economic activities have brought about building development. lt can be seen particularly in mountainous areas where narrow stream valleys have frequently been built over. Mountainous streams can be characterised by a great variabiliÿ of water stages and a high culmination of flood flows. High slopes of the riverbeds cause a rapid growth of velociÿ during high flows. Nowadays protection against damages caused [SOURCE: Page_110]

107 [SOURCE: Page_110]

by flooding is a prioriÿ. Specific features of the artificial channel construction determine the conditions of its application. They are as follows: -  full protection of the adjacent areas against floods, -  building development close to the riverbed, -  high slope of the riverbed, -  high velociÿ and shear stress values for the bed-load. ln southern Poland, the first technical solution of the culvert appeared in the twenties and thirties of the 20h century. The Krzywiczanka Stream training and the Bysinka Stream in My§lenice are ÿpical examples of the culvert solution. However, over the years it turned out that those solutions did not fulfil the expected demands. Hence, the culvert constructions have been modified several times by now (Ratomski, 2000). The aim of the paper is to present a critical review of the applied culvert constructions: - in the longitudinal profile, - in the shape changes of the cross-section. [SOURCE: Page_111]

2. Description of culvert constructions The system of culvert building is based on the lining of the whole cross-section with material resistant to high flow velociÿ. The cross-section is dimensioned for the hundred years retum discharge (Qr",r). The first culvert constructions were made of grouted stone. Their cross-section shapes were: circular (the Slonka Stream in Rabka), trapezoidal (the Kryniczanka Stream in Krynica) and parabolic (the Bysinka Stream in My§lenice). The construction of the culvert on the Bysinka Stream is shown in Fot.1. This solution was applied because of the following reasons: - damage of the adjacent area caused by flooding, - neighbourhood of the state road Krakôw- Zakopane, - built- over area close to the riverbed. Such a solution of the culvert construction guaranteed the outflow. However, these constructions quite often caused many negative effects, such as: -  high water velociÿ in the culvert, -  low water level during the longJasting low flow, -  inappropriate water thermal condition and shortage of water aeration, -  disappearance of flora and fauna, -  neglect of aestetic values in mountainuos lanscape. ln thJ fifties and sixties of the 20th century the âbove solution was very often applied. The visible elements of culvert constructions were made of the stone slab facing placed on the cement mortar [Fig.1]. Current directives still recommend circular and trapezoidal shapes of the culver in spite of all the defects of these constructions. The culvert in Lomnica and Rabka [Fot.2] are ÿpical examples of the described solution. Not much attention was paid to the ecological aspect of the proposed design, as it was not a prioriÿ at that time. [SOURCE: Page_111]

108 [SOURCE: Page_111]

Figure.l. The shape of ÿpical culvert cross-section. [SOURCE: Page_112]

ln the seventies tourism and recreation development in the mountain area led to the situation in which streams started to be viewed as fluvial hydrosystem. Hence, the efforts were directed to environmentally sensitive designing (Petts, Calow, 1996, Designing instructions..., 1979). Within stream channels habitats are in large part determined by a range of hydraulic and morphological variables: flow, depth, velociÿ, shear stress, and channel width, depth, slope (Designing instructions..., 1979). The culvert constructions of the seventies and later periods were as close to nature as possible. Banks were designed with varied slopes in order to simulate natural cross-sections. ln the main channel      of the riverbed the separate low- flow channel for low flow is quite often designed. lt guarantees high water stages throughout the whole time [Fig.2]. The concrete wall-boards and concrete slabs with the thickness of 70 cm were replaced by precast callural slabs. These kind of slabs enabled vegetation growh that could hide artificial concrete elements. The culverts in Tarnôw on the Wqtok Stream and the Tzemeénianka Stream are ÿpical examples of a described solution [Fot..3, Fot.4]. [SOURCE: Page_112]

Figure.2. The culvert cross-section with the channel for low flow. [SOURCE: Page_112]

These constructions are characterized by: -  filling the whole width of constructed riverbed with water, -  maintaining appropriate water depths during the low flow, -  possibiliÿ of vegetation growth which can hide the precast callural slabs of the bank slopes protection, -  restoring better conditions for fauna, -  possibility of fish migration. [SOURCE: Page_112]

109 [SOURCE: Page_112]

Figure 3. The longitudinal profile of culvert [SOURCE: Page_113]

Figure 4. The longitudinal profile of culvert with the steps [SOURCE: Page_113]

Figure 5. ïhe longitudinal profile of culvert with three possibilities of slab construction. [SOURCE: Page_113]

The best solution, apart from the well-designed cross-section can also be obtained also by a properly planned longitudinal profile. The previous solutions of culvert constructions were based on establishing the uniform slope of the riverbed. This slope was usually equal to the mean slope of the designed section [Fig.3]. However, such a solution of the longitudinal profile, although having     a  well-designed section, had          a negative impact on the environment. [SOURCE: Page_113]

110 [SOURCE: Page_113]

ln order to minimise these negative impacts another solution of longitudinal profile has been proposed. Series of low steps located along the riverbeds caused the decrease of the water-surhce slope [Fig.4]. The conveyance is calculated for the slope determined by the sills.The above mentioned solution of the culvert construction only partly reduced the negative impact on the environment (Bergmann, Schiechtl, 1999). The noteworthy culvert construction with an interesting solution of a longitudinal profile is placed on the \Merchomla Stream -the tributary of the Dunajec River [Fot.s]. The main objective of that construction is applying the system of steps with increased height above the culvert slab. A cascade of steps gives the opportuniÿ to form small basins above each step. The advantages of this solution are as follows: -  guarantee ofthe channel flood-flows, -  guarantee of longJasting permanent water stages in the designed section, -  appropriate conditions for flora and fauna development, -  improving water qualiÿ (thermalcondition and water aeration), -  keeping the culvert construction in harmony with the landscape, -  water for recreation (small basins) and water supplies (intakes). The proposed solutions can be applied with any shape of a cross-section [Fig.5]. [SOURCE: Page_114]

3. Pro-ecological solutions The presentation of culvert construction solutions leads to the analysis of the culvert influence on the mountainous streams and their habitat. Stones and concrete are ÿpical materials used for the culvert construction. Such kinds of artificial materials are inappriopriate for flora and fauna development. The riverbed and bank protection made of concrete slabs makes water the only natural element of the stream. Nevertheless, this kind of construction is sometimes the best solution for protection the adjacent areas against the flood. Hence, it should be as close to nature as possible. The culvert shape that is closest to nature can be obtained by designing the most natural form of it. A trapezoidal shape of the culvert with the varied slope of banks corresponds  to     a natural shape of the cross-section. ln the sufficiently broad channel bed, the separate low-flow channel for low flows should be designed. lts course, which is unsymmetrical in relation to the main axis of the designed stream route, leads to a more natural character of the solution. Moreover, such a solution guarantees proper water levels throughout the years and better conditions for fauna. The materialwhich forms the bank slopes protection is also important. The banks should be formed by precast callural slabs (for example "PP-elements" - proposed by J. Kostecki or callural slabs - "PK") which are characterized by a high resistance to high flow velociÿ. They make the whole culvert construction light (in weight) and more elastic (Woloszyn, 1994). A few years of exploitation of this kind of construction shows that filling the empÿ spaces of the collural slabs with the fertile land æuses the fast vegetation growth. Finally, the vegetation hides the precast callural slabs of the bank protection and is also the habitat for fauna and flora. [SOURCE: Page_114]

111 [SOURCE: Page_114]

The condition that the whole channel bed should be filled with water all the time is the most important. lt guarantees the proper conditions for fish and amphibian existence. The overdeepenings can be frcrmed by precast callural slabs or concrete slabs (Starmach, 2000). However, the slabs should be irregularly undulated in order to form the habitat for fauna in overdeepenings filled with sand. Moreover, single stones placed on the channel bed slabs give shelter for fish and amphibians. The previous system of culvert building precluded fish existence and migration. The main reason for that was the fact, that the whole channel bed was not filled with water during the longJasting low water stages (especially during the winter). There was also high velociÿ during the high flow. A construction of a small channel for a low flow should be formed in the riverbed in order to guarantee a suitable water stage for fish during the period of low flow. Series of low steps located in the whole designed section causes decreasing of the water-table slope. These solutions improve the conditions for fauna. The shelters, placed in the steps and in wall - board of the culvert, serve as a hide for fish even during the high flow. The proposed pro-ecological solutions make the culvert construction close to nature. [SOURCE: Page_115]

4. Conclusion The system of culvert building in the mountainous streams and rivers is indispensable. Only this kind of stream training protects the adjacent highly built over areas against the negative effects of the flood-flow with the culmination of Q$_1$.7.. The designed system of the culvert should fulfil all the requirements of the construction, material and the channel capaciÿ. However, it is also important to take into account the aesthetics of the construction and its conformabiliÿ with local landscape architecture [Fot.6, Fot.7]. The possibiliÿ of the designed constructions should be nowadays also a guarantee of suitable conditions for fauna and flora of the stream and the whole adjacent area. The aim of the paper is to present a critical description of the culvert constructions applied in the Upper Vistula catchment. On the basis of the presented solutions of culvert constructions, it is easy to note that, it is impossible to have only one solution that is always correct. lt depends on many factors (aesthetics, construction functionaliÿ, adaptation to the local conditions) and it is impossible to take all of them into account. lt shoud be noted that the higher the cost of investmtent are, the better better is the qualiÿ of it. The constructions that are(were) used are characterised by a long-term of shock absorption. Hence, the inputs are incomparable with the obtained benefits. The aesthetics of the construction and securiÿ provided against floods can bring economic development to the adjacent areas. [SOURCE: Page_115]

112 [SOURCE: Page_115]

English summary Human errors and random nature processes very often resulted in dams and dikes disasters. Therefore there is a continuous work on improvement of technical rules, laws, procedures and organization related to dikes and dams safeÿ. They concem all stages of a structure's "life": planning, designing, realization and maintenance, which all are equally important. The present paper has for objective to describe, analyze and compare various aspects related to dam and dike safeÿ, in Poland and France. lt presents in both countries the state administrations, public and private organizations which dikes and dams belong or are submitted to, Next, dikes and dams classification is characterized. This helps as a basis to present the procedures, administration structures, law acts and rules for the planning, building, designing and maintenance, and also the methods for minimizing hazards and failure risks. Finally Polish and French laws and technical rules concerning dams and dikes dimensioning are presented. [SOURCE: Page_122]

Résumé français : Comparaison des lois, procédures, organisations et règles techniques relatives à la sécurité des barages et des digues en Pologne et en France [SOURCE: Page_122]

Les erreurs humaines et les processus aléatoires de la nature ont très souvent eu comme conséquence des catastrophes sur des barrages et des digues. Par conséquent, il y a un travail continu sur I'amélioration des règles techniques, des lois, des proédures et de I'organisation, liées à la sécurité des digues et banages. Ces aspects concernent toutes les étapes de la "vie" d'une structure : planification, conception, réalisation et entretien, qui sont toutes également importantes. Le présent article a pour objectif de décrire, d'analyser et comparer divers aspects relatifs à la sécurité des banages et des digues, en Pologne et en France. ll présente dans un premier temps les Administrations de I'Etat et les organisations publiques et privées auxquelles les digues et les barrages appartiennent ou sont soumis, dans chacun des deux pays. Puis, la classification des digues et des barrages est présentée. Ceci sert de base pour présenter ensuite les procédures, les structures administratives, les lois et règlements relatift à la planification, à la construction, à la conception et à I'entretien, ainsi que les méthodes destinées à réduire au minimum les risques de rupture. Enfin, les lois et règles techniques polonaises et françaises au sujet du dimensionnement des banages et des digues sont présentées. [SOURCE: Page_122]

118 [SOURCE: Page_122]

1. lntroduction Human errors and random nature processes very often resulted in dams and dikes disasters. We can mention the catastrophe in Malpasset where 500 people were killed because of the wave that was created after the dam's failure, or dikes catastrophes during 2002 floods in Poland and France where many people perished and moreover very large flood looses occurred. ln Poland lhere are 174 higher dams (they belong to classes from I to lll; chapter 2,1 Polish dams and dikes classification), 650 lower ones (class lV), and almost 9700 km of dikes. ln France there are about 300 dams higher than 20m, 200 dams lower than 20m but nonetheless interesting public safety and from 7500 to 12000km of dikes (their amount isn't sure because they are still being identified). These values show the scale of the possible risk for large inhabited areas in both countries. So, there is a continuous work on improving technical rules, laws, procedures and organization related to dikes and dams safety. They concern all stages of a structure's "life": planning, designing, realization and maintenance, which all are equally important. But in many €ses particular elements and aspects of these stages have different solutions in both countries. lt is very interesting, or even necessary, to compare them between themselves in order to chose the best one, or suitable one depending on needs. Therefore this article was written to present this comparison, as a result of collaboration between Polish Cracow Universiÿ of Technology and French Cemagref. [SOURCE: Page_123]

2. Dams and dikes classification. There are different attitudes towards dams and dikes classification in France and Poland. \/ÿtrile in France for dikes and dams there is a classification of works interesting or not public safeÿ, in Poland both dams and dikes are classified into one of four classes depending on different indicators. [SOURCE: Page_123]

119 [SOURCE: Page_123]

2.1 Polish dams and dikes classification. ln Poland, dams and dikes are classified into one of four classes of hydraulic structures. A structure belongs to the class which has the lowest indicator pointed by any from the checked ones. There are nine indicators and, in Table 1, the five most important ones related to public safeÿ are presented. Class of construction defines : technical conditions for calculating flows, coefficients to structure stabiliÿ analysis, minimal freeboard, but also limits of preliminary design studies and main project studies, level of monitoring equipment and range of control. [SOURCE: Page_124]

Table 1. Simplified classification of Polish dams and dikes [SOURCE: Page_124]

2.2 French dams and dikes classification. Dams and dikes in France can be classified into a group interesting or not public safeÿ. A dam is classified as "interesting public safeÿ" if it is higher than 20m, or lower than 20m but whose failure can cause hazard for inhabited area or important communication ways. For dams interesting public safeÿ there are obligations and control rules that concern the building stage and, later, structure maintenance. For dams which are not interesting public safeÿ, the only general law is about owner responsibility for looses to any outside party, including particularly the case of failure. Classification of French dikes was recently introduced by ministerialcircular on 6 August 2003. On the base of a national dikes inventory, which is being realized (starting from 1999), and already prepared atlases of submersible areas, all existing dikes must be classified as interesting or not public safeÿ. Of course , this classification also will be applied to dikes which will be built in the future. To the group of dikes important for public safeÿ belongs the ones which failure threatens people's life, important communication or telecommunication lines, or can create serious pollution. [SOURCE: Page_124]

3. Structures concerning dikes and dams. The main difference between French and Polish organisational structures concerning dams and dikes is that the Polish ones are much more complicated. ln France there are three ministries: Transport, lndustry and Environment in which area of responsibiliÿ are dams and dikes. Each one has its own control service organisation. Control service is supported by special technical service. For example, dams for flood protection, alimentation in drinking water, irrigation, sport and recreation, production of hydroelectric power < 45001«V and also flood dikes and dikes of irrigation canals are under control of the ministry in charge of environment. Water Police, which is their service control, receives technical assistance from Cemagref (Agricultural and Environmental Engineering Research Center). ln France there are three levels of territorial division: State -> Region -> Department -> Communes. ln every France region (or department), Water Police is subordinate to prefecture. The prefecture is responsible for dams and dikes project authorization or declaration approval. Additionally in France, for dams higher than 20m, was created (in 1966, six years after dam's catastrophe in Malpasset) a special inter-ministry committee of specialists called CTPB (Dams Permanent Technical Committee) to check and give advice on every project (building or big repairs). ln Poland there are also three levels of territorial division: State -> voivodshipes -> local district -> communes. On every level of this structure there are both a state administration and a self-government. And almost all of these levels of administrative structures are engaged in procedures of dam or dike project authorization. lt would be very long to write about them all, so only voivodship, level, as the most important, will be presented here. There are voivoda - representative of state administration, and voivodship, marshal - representative of self govemment. Voivoda among other things is responsible for "water supply and sewage effluent disposal consent" and "building license" in the stage of any dams and dikes project authorization. The state is the owner of most of the flood dikes in Poland. Voivodship marshal is its representative in their management. He authorizes Lands Reclamation Authoriÿ (\ /ZiR) to realize this function. Moreover there are three other important institutions for dams and dikes in Poland. The first one is Water Management Board. lt divides Poland into six regions, which generally corresponds to the catchments of main Polish rivers. Directors of regional Water Management Departments, among other things, are responsible for agreements conceming water economy ventures, water management planning and controlling, water cadaster, acting of owner to hydraulic structure (there are also dams and dikes) subordinated to them, coordination of flood control measures. lnspectors of Water Management Departments controls water law application. The second one is General Office of Building. lt is a central unit of state administration in charge of architectural and building administration and building inspection. ln these maters voivoda of each voivodship, is subordinated to General Office of Building, which by means of: a) Voivodship, Architectural-Building Department (among other things), gives building license and structure use permit. b) Voivodship, lnspector of Building Control controls building law fulfillment. [SOURCE: Page_125]

121 [SOURCE: Page_125]

The last one is Committee for Technical Assessment of Dams (OT$_1$«) in lnstitute of Meteorology and Water Management (National Research lnstitution). OTI(Z is a service control for the dams of I and ll class. lt realizes every year control visits for every dam in classes I and ll. [SOURCE: Page_126]

4. Stages in the life of dams and dikes and their importance to safety All elements of all dams' and flood dikes' "life" stages are important for their safeÿ Here are presented some chosen ones. [SOURCE: Page_126]

4.1 Stage of designing ln France every project of dam or flood dike must be authorized or declared in prefecture. lf necessary, prefecture sends design documentation to other institutions (like, for example, service control) where it is checked or where opinions about it are expressed, and then it goes back to prefecture. ln a project's authorization act, the Prefect can add obligations that have to be fulfilled. For dams higher than 20m the project must also be checked by the special committee CTPB. This committee checks the dam project. lts advices can concem the project but also the building stage and maintenance. Here also should be mentioned the French Committee on Large Dams (CFGB), which is affiliated to lnternational Committee on Large Dams (ICOLD). lt is a scientific socieÿ made from experts in all domains related to dams, which produces scientific recommendations for them. lt has a large influence on technical guidelines concerning dams in France. ln Poland, as it was written in chapter 3, dams' or dikes' project authorization is subject to some authorizations in different parts of administrative structures. The most important are "water supply and sewage effluent disposal consent" which states rules, conditions and obligations for water use, and 'building license" which is given after control if all designing solutions in the project are in accordance to the law. Building license is the base to start building. There is no special commission to check project as CTPB in France, but every project (also dam and dike) must be checked by a second design engineer. The elements of design documentation are similar in both countries. There are: Environmental impact report, P§ect, Geotechnical studies (in France this is a part of project) and Studies of structure failure consequences (not for every project, in France, mainly for dams and dikes interesting Public safeÿ). [SOURCE: Page_126]

4.2 Stage of building ln France with the authorization of the dam's or dike's p§ect, the Prefect can also give (as it was described in last paragraph) obligations which must be fulfilled. All conditions established by CTPB for dams which are subordinate to it must also be realized, if the Prefect asks for it. At all time a dam's or dike's building is supervised by a design engineer. The Control service can control the area of building whenever it wants, but in case of a dam, first of all, they check foundations before they are covered, and finally they check the finished dam to agree for first filling. These control procedures applied to [SOURCE: Page_126]

122 [SOURCE: Page_126]

dams concern only dams interesting public safeÿ. For the other ones there are no obligated rules. lt is left to the control service appreciation. After dike completion or dam first filling, reports for the control service must be prepared. For dams higher than 20m a special report for CTPB is also necessary. ln Poland as in France dam's and dike's building are supervised by a design engineer. But additionally there is also an investor supervisor who confirms realization of works (particularly that which are covered) and confirms also faulÿ works corrections. ln case of a dam first filling, they both belong, with other necessary specialists, to the "first filling commission" which controls dams' first filling. Voivodship lnspector of Building Control has power to control building whenever he wants, particularly in case of a building law not being fulfilled. He is also a member of the commission which controls a dike when the building is finished or a dam before its first filling. [SOURCE: Page_127]

4.3 Stage of maintenance and methods for minimizinghazard and failure risk [SOURCE: Page_127]

4.3.{ Stage of dike's maintenance. The new circular from August 2003, which was already mentioned in this article, changed attitude to dikes maintenance in France. French dikes may now (as dams) be classified into a group of dikes interesting or not public safeÿ. Rules of their classification are presented in the chapter 2,2 "French dam's and dike's classification'. Because many dikes haven't been controlled for years until now, some necessary steps after their classification must be realized (they are also described by new circular). The first step is the owner identification . The next step is complement of dike's documentation: administrative, technical, management. Then takes place a diagnostic visit in presence of control service, which results in obligation of necessary repairs. And after a time of this "orders', the owner must prepare the dike's book of maintenance rules and procedures which should then be approved by control service and used by himself. ln the new circular also are introduced very important obligations concerning dike control visit. Owner has to organize at least one dike's visit each year with his contractor (consultant engineer), or two times per year if there were high water levels. Another important rule is the organization of a control visit, at least one every two years, in presence of Water Police with a complete report (written by owneds contractor) about observations and notices. ln Poland dike's maintenance looks a little different because Lands Reclamation Authoriÿ (\ ZiR), which manages most of dikes in Poland, has it own control structures. The sections of dikes are division between \AZiR's inspectors, who are obligated to visit them in spring and autumn and who prepare post visit report. Voivodship, lnspectors of Building Control controls if control visit and report are done (at least one per year). [SOURCE: Page_127]

123 [SOURCE: Page_127]

4.3.2 Stage of dam's maintenance There is an interesting difference between French and Polish dams maintenance. It concerns control visits. ln France dams important for public safeÿ have an annual control visit which is realized by the control service in the presence of owner and owne/s contractor (consultant engineer). Owne/s contractor also prepares an annual report. Every second year this report includes a deeper analysis of monitoring measurements. Finally, every ten years the visit is to be done with emptied reservoir (except for justified exceptions). ln Poland control visits are realized every year by the dam's owner contractor (engineer), who also prepares post visit report. Every fifth year, as it was written in chapter about Polish structure, OTI<Z controls the correctness of these annual control visits for class I and ll dams . Moreover, Voivodship lnspectors of Building Control controls if every annual report is done for all dam's classes. Surely it is a very good idea to organize every ten years a visit with reservoir empÿing, as it really shows a dam's condition, particularly of its usually hidden parts. [SOURCE: Page_128]

4.3.3 The methods for minimizing hazard and failure risk ln the case of dams we can distinguish two ÿpes of risks, which are generally connected with two different maintenance states. First, for normal dams maintenance there is a risk for people who can be in the area downstream from the dam, caused by sudden water level increase because of dam operation. ln both countries this level of risk is reduced in a similar way by notice boards and local communiÿ information, but moreover sound signal is used in Poland. Much more dangerous is the second ÿpe of risk, which is connected to dam's failure. ln FraÀce for dams higher than 20m and with capaciÿ bigger than 15 hm^3 the Prefect prepares Plans for Particular lntervention (PPl). PPI presents menaces created in case of dam's catastrophe. lt enumerates the consequences of this situation, but also states what must be installed or organized by the dam's owner to reduce dam's failure consequences(particularly sirens), and measures to alarm diffusion. The base of PPI are risk analysis and analysis of created flood wave. Two other plans are also prepared by Prefect. Plan ORSEC (Organization of help) concerns public and private resources, which should be used in case of any catastrophe, and conditions to use them by pertinent authorities. PSS - Plan of Special Protection is prepared to protect against risks related to dams which are not submitted to PPl. ln Poland for each level of the country division its local authorities should prepare assessment of flood protection. But in case of elemental disaster or technical catastrophe they also lead the actions to limit its results with the help of Crises Management Co-ordination Groups. They prepare Plan for Crisis Management which characterizes: actions for menaces monitoring, state and private services, organization, institution and measures that can be used to limit range of elemental disaster influence and lessen its results, it also presents procedures of cooperation between them and procedures for all actions listed by Plan. One of the basis necessary to prepare Plan are instructions for actions and measures to take in case of dam's failure. They are prepared after dam's first filling. [SOURCE: Page_128]

124 [SOURCE: Page_128]

5. Technical rules related to dams and dikes dimensioning ln this chapter are presented some chosen aspects of the technical rules related to dams and dikes dimensioning in Poland and France. Elements like: design flows, methods and rules related to outlet works discharge and inter dikes distance discharge calculations, safeÿ height of water construction above levels of water and stability analysis are often calculated by different methods in both countries. A very interesting difference is that in Poland technical rules related to hydraulic structure dimensioning are part of the law, while in France they aren't. ln Poland, technical rules are imposed by ministerial decree about technical conditions for hydraulic structures, which was prepared by a team of specialists, on the basis of actual hydraulic engineering knowledge and knowledge about regional conditions. ln France technical rules are not defined by a law, but rather by general consensus among technical experts (including work by the committees CFGB, CTPB, ...). Law defines general principles, and to what control process structures are submitted or not, but does not rule out technical calculations. So, in France, an engineer is more free as regards hydraulic structure (dams and dikes) general designing and dimensioning. Moreover, comparison of French technical rules for dams higher than 20m with the same ones in Poland is very difficult, because every case of such dams in France is considered in an individual way by CTPB (CTPB and its working are described in chapter 3,2 about stage of designing). The situation as regards dikes is very similar for dams, there is also no official technical rules. New laws concerning dikes maintenance and their classification were recently introduced, so technical rules consensus among technical experts are stillto be established. [SOURCE: Page_129]

5.1 Design flows One of the basic parameter needed to design a dam or a dike is a flow, aptly called design flow. ln Poland, as well as in France, the concept of design flows usually is characterized by its occurrence probabiliÿ (in France flood retum period is rather used). [SOURCE: Page_129]

5.1.1 Poland There are two kinds of design flood in Poland, and for both, the outlets and spillways of the dam or the inter dikes cross section are checked. These are : -Base flood Qr- it is the flood with defined by law probability of appearance for normal maintenance of the hydraulic structure. lt is used to determine: - Maximum Water Leveland dimension the spillways and outlets for dams, - parameters of safety inter dikes distance cross section -Control flood Qr- lt is maximal possible design flow. lts probabiliÿ of appearance is defined by law and it is lower than the one for base flood. lt is used to calculate exceptional conditions of the hydraulic structures maintenance. It is used to calculate discharges by spillways and outlets or inter dikes cross- section without any risk of dam or dike failure, but with the possibiliÿ of some damages. [SOURCE: Page_129]

125 [SOURCE: Page_129]

Depending on the class of the hydraulic structure (look at chapter 2) the base flood and control flood have a different value for their occurrence probabiliÿ. lt is presented in table 2. Table 2. Occurrence probabiliÿ of base floods and control floods for dams and dikes in Poland [SOURCE: Page_130]

5.1.2 France ln France, there are also, for important hydraulic works, two kinds of design flow which are taken into the calculation of dams. These are: design flood and safety flood. Design flood is the flood with the smallest occurrence probability considered in the reservoir. lt is used to determine the Maximum water level and to dimension the spillway(s). For dams, the value of the design flood's occurrence probability depends on the parameter H2W lwhere H- dam height in metres, V- reservoir volume in hm^3), for embankment dam, is presented in table 3. But when a dam belongs to a group of dams interesting public safeÿ (look at chapter 2), the minimal occurrence probabiliÿ should never be less than 0,1. [SOURCE: Page_130]

H.{V                              <5           5to^30         30 to 100     100 to 700              >700 Occunence probabiliÿ of the design flood p%          1,0/100         0,5t^200       0,1/1000      0,05/2000          0,01/10000 I flood retum period in vears. r a b t e 3' o c c u r re n c e r'ff;Z: y":i,::                         F re n c h e a fth f i t t d a m i:        ;l; ; i :i::,::: ;n :, [SOURCE: Page_130]

(where: H: dam height in m, V reservoir volume in millions of m^3) Safeÿ flood- this is the worst flood that can occur to the dam without any risk of failure. For example for a dam with an impervious core, it is the flood for which the reseryoir water level will rise to the level of the core. And it is important to say again that, for dam higher then 20m, occurrence probabiliÿ of the design flood and safeÿ flood is definitely established after individual analysis by CTPB. [SOURCE: Page_130]

126 [SOURCE: Page_130]

For French dikes there are no official rules now and no guidelines about design flows either. But generally the attitude to this matter should be similar to the one towards dams. So we can distinguish a design flood for which protected area is not protected anymore (flooding by weirs), but the dike is not destroyed. Second flood is similar to the safety flood for dams: it is the worst flood that can occur in the space between the dikes without any risk of their failure. ln France the design flood's occurrence probability for dikes is usually assumed higher than for dams, ifs from 10o/o to 0,1o/o. For more important industrial, and inhabited areas or communication ways (roads or trains) these values can be smaller. The most important conclusion concerning flood's occurrence probability for dams and dikes in Poland and France is that in both countries it is connected to level of risk for the sidestream area and balanced by cost of safeÿ protection. [SOURCE: Page_131]

5.2 Methods of Design flows calculations The next logical step after presentation of flovr/s occurrence probabiliÿ is the presentation of Design flows calculation methods. They are very similar in both countries. We can distinguish: -gmpirica formutas                            - ln both countries these are used for small dams and dikes if their failure is not susceptible to cause heavy damages and if there is no other hydrological data. -Method of an analoqv to a similar catchment - ln Poland this method is considered to be more accurate than empirical formulas, and for dams where there is no hydrological data it is used to lengthen the data series. ln France it isn't used very often, except for smallcatchments (<500km^2) -fne pfutp-pUf metnoO - (Probable Maximum Precipitation - Probable Maximum Flood). lt is an American method, which consists in calculating the Probable Maximum Flood on the base of observed precipitation data and maximizing the meteorological parameters linked to rainfall (PNP). lt is rather rarely used in France and Poland. -Statistical mg!:noclS-   There is no official statistical method now in Poland and France and different methods are used. They are constantly in progress. ln Poland the old official method, based on Pearson ÿpe lll and quantiles method, lost its validiÿ. ln France the most popular method is now GRADEX method, where the main parameter is proportional to the standard deviation of maximum precipitation values; it gives highly different results from the previous one, which is quite frowned upon. - -Modelinq There are many models in both countries without any official rules. These are hydrological or hydraulic models or combination of both ÿpes. Models of flood scenario simulation are also used, which have the advantage to produce realistic flood scenario, better adapted to outlet dimensioning than rough maximum flood calculation. [SOURCE: Page_131]

5.3 Methods and rules for dam's spillways and outlets discharge, and also inter dikes cross-sections discharge calculations. Accurate hydraulic computation of dam's outlets and spillways and inter dikes cross-section for design flows are significant as regards dams and dikes safety. [SOURCE: Page_131]

127 [SOURCE: Page_131]

Assumptions and conditions taken into consideration in these calculations are very important. [SOURCE: Page_132]

5.3.1 Polish computation rules of inter dikes crosssection discharge ln case of dike designing, the engineer must check two conditions. One for normal dike's maintenance: the freeboard for base flood (Qr) must be higher than the minimal required one (Table 4). The second condition for exceptional dike's maintenance: the freeboard for control flood (Qd must also be higher than the minimal required one (Table 4). The first condition does not seem to be necessary because control flood is atways bigger than base flood. The explanation is that the minimal required freeboard for control flood is not the same one as for base flood, but it is lower, as can be seen in Table 4. [SOURCE: Page_132]

Safeÿ height of Polish hydraulic structures crest above water level [SOURCE: Page_132]

5.3.2 French computation rules of inter levee distance discharge The calculation procedure for dikes in France is the same as in Poland. The design engineer checks if for design flood and safeÿ flood; the necessary freeboard are left. But as it was already written there are no official rules for design flood and safeÿ flood, neither for minimal freeboards for dikes in France. So assessment of their minimal height belongs to design engineer and control service. [SOURCE: Page_132]

5.3.3 Polish and French calculation rules for dam's outlets and spillways discharge The calculation for dam's spillways and outlets discharge in Poland and France has many rules and conditions which must be fulfilled. They concem outlets and spillways dimensioning and their structural conditions, as well as minimal freeboards. Only the most important ones will be presented here. [SOURCE: Page_132]

128 [SOURCE: Page_132]

Because in Poland law defines most of these conditions, this is a good base to compare them with the French ones where there are no official guidelines for design engineer. Moreover, for dams higher than 20m they are checked by CTPB. ln a general way, about these calculations, the first step in both countries is assumption of Maximal water level (MWL) in a reservoir. lt depends on the valley geomorphologic and hydrological parameters and dam's use. Then M\Â/L and associated minimal freeboard give the level of the dam's crest. Simultaneously, it is necessary to calculate the dam's spiltways and outlets dimensions for design flows discharge and with respect of the safeÿ freeboard. [SOURCE: Page_133]

This introduction can be developed to the following computational points: [SOURCE: Page_133]

o ln Poland; first an assumption of maximal level water and freeboard calculation with and withoutwaving. The value of minimal freeboard is presented in Table 4. ln France there are several guideline formulas for minimum freeboard. One of them is: :1ï2lf'511t^414 (Hdam height in m, V-reservoir volume in millions of m^3 :) but there waving should also be taken into consideration. o ln Poland; checking conditions for normal dam's maintenance (for base flood). ln the calculation it must be taken into account that some of the bottom outlets, galleries, siphon spiltways and power tunnels don't work (they are closed and this amount is defined in ministerial decree). For example for a number of bottom outlets from 1 to 3, one is calculated as closed, from 4 to 6, two are closed, and from 7 to 9, three are closed. Moreover if there are spillways with gates, one of them must be calculated as closed (its mechanism could be broken or blocked). For these assumptions minimal required freeboard is checked (Table 4) . ln France; this is calculated differently in each dam. Generally minimal freeboard for design flood with and without waving is checked. o ln Poland; checking of conditions for exceptional dam's maintenance (for control flood). All spillways and outflows are calculated as opened. The minimal required freeboard is taken from the Table 4. ln France; conditions of safeÿ flood discharge are found for each dam by individual analysis. o There are also some important and interesting constructional and dimensional structural conditions in Poland and guidelines in France for spillways and outlets. One of the most important rule for design floods discharge calculation in Poland is that a spillway must discharge at least 80% of the base flood. Another one is that if water lifting is higher than 2m and capaciÿ of reservoir > 200 000 cubic meters, the bottom outlet must have two pipes at least, with possibiliÿ of repairing one of them without influence on the other one's efficiency. There is no similar obligatory rule in France, and some higher dams have only one bottom outlet. tn both countries bottom outlets are considered as necessary for empÿing reservoiis dams. The upstream-downstream bottom outlet slope in France should be 2 1o/o while in Poland >0,2%. [SOURCE: Page_133]

129 [SOURCE: Page_133]

5.4 Stabiliÿ analysis in Poland and France ln Poland as in France there are various methods used for stabiliÿ analysis. But as it was already mentioned Polish technical rules concerning stabiliÿ analysis are characterized by norms and ministerial decree, while in France there is no official rules for them. For smaller embankment dams in Poland as well as in France older methods of Bishop, Fellenius or limit state are used. ln Poland the limit state method is well developed and defined by law. More precise coefficients are used, which in detail allow to calculate different æses of stabiliÿ analysis. For example, among other ones are used special coefficient consequences of hydraulic structure destroying yn, ând coefficient loads configuration yr-c. A very important aspect of stabiliÿ calculation is the problem of an inaccuracy in measurements and error propagation which does not depend on design engineer, but which are able to cause that the structure can be unstable , although fulfilling the inequaliÿ of state's method. Therefore in Poland this error is estimated essentially for dams I and ll class. As regards the stabiliÿ of graviÿ dams all necessary cases for sliding, stabiliÿ against overturning and internal stabiliÿ are calculated in both countries. For the higher graviÿ dam as well as fill dams finite elements method is used. ln Poland there are no arch dams, while in France there is a large amount of them and their stabiliÿ is usually calculated by finite elements method. The main difference in calculating a dam's stabiliÿ between Poland and France is that in France the earthquake influence is additionally taken into consideration. [SOURCE: Page_134]

6 Conclusion There are different classifications of dams and dikes in both countries. In France classiÿing into a group interesting public safeÿ provides the owner and the control service with organizational rules concerning structure control, at the time of its building and during its maintenance. A very interesting idea is the control of every dam project higher than 20 m by a special committee of specialists: CTPB. lt gives the maximum possible control for the dam project. ln France there are no official rules concerning technical designing for dams and dikes. This is why the responsibiliÿ of the design engineer and control service seems to be higher than in Poland where law, simultaneously with classification of dam or dike, defines rules concerning organization of control but also technical rules. They guarantee structure's safeÿ because minimum technical parameters of safe§ for each class of structure is imposed. But at the same time it restricts the engineefs designing fteedom, as well as the possibiliÿ of introducing improvements to design methods. There are also different organizations (as for exemple control service) concerning dams and dikes in both countries. Notwithstanding these differences, all stages of dams' "life" are well controlled in Poland and France. ln the case of dikes, in Poland control is already ongoing while in France a law from August 2003 is a new base to organize a good dikes control system. [SOURCE: Page_134]

English summary To understand the long-term behaviour of a dam, it is essential to carefully interpret the measured data, in order to be able to distinguish between the effects of faclors such as drift, irreversible events and the ageing of the dam and the effects of other factors not involving ageing processes. The Hydrostatic-Season-Time (HSI) model is presented in this paper. The main components of the basic model are: the effect of the reservoir level; the seasonal effect; and time drift. The rainfall must be taken into account when analysing hydraulic related measurements. Piezometric levels of a homogeneous embankment dam (15.5 m high) are analysed, as well as variations of the measurements of three 3D crackmeters, installed in a spillway. These case studies are interpreted. [SOURCE: Page_137]

Résumé français : Analyse de mesures d'auscultation de barrages en tere [SOURCE: Page_137]

Pour comprendre le comportement à long terme d'un banage, il est essentiel d'interpréter soigneusement les mesures d'auscultation, afin de pouvoir distinguer les effets des facteurs tels que la dérive, les événements inéversibles et le vieillissement du banage, des effets d'autres facteurs n'impliquant pas des processus vieillissant. Le modèle Hydrostatique-Saison-Temps (HST) est présenté dans cet article. Ses composants principaux de base sont: I'effet du niveau de réservoir; I'effet saisonnier; et la dérive dans le temps. Les précipitations doivent être prises en considération pour l'analyse de mesures liées à l'hydraulique. Les niveaux piézométriques d'un banage en terre homogène de 15,5 m de hauteur sont analysés, ainsi que les variations des mesures de trois vinchons, installés dans un coursier de déversoir de crues. Le résultat de ces études de cas sont interprétés. [SOURCE: Page_137]
